I just can't see how I could be completely wrong when it's right there on my face. I don't think they've changed much. It feels like it's just always been this way. Day in and day out, it's just the exact same things I'm focusing on. So, yeah, I really do think it actually looks that bad, even if other people try to tell me it doesn't.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people can see the flaws in your face and hairline that you're concerned about?\n\nPatient: Well, my coworkers tell me they don't see what I'm talking about when I bring it up. But... I just can't stop thinking about it. That's actually why I'm here. I feel like when I'm at work, under those bright restaurant lights, my hairline and my face just make me look so old and strange. So even though people say they don't notice it, I feel like they have to be able to see it. I just can't stop focusing on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your hairline and face actually look normal, but you just can't see them that way?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean, I guess it's possible, since everyone keeps telling me it's fine. But... it's just really hard for me to believe that. When I look in the mirror, it doesn't look normal to me at all. It just looks wrong. So even if it is possible, it's just not what I see.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your hairline and face could be completely wrong, even if it feels absolutely right to you?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess maybe? If I really force myself to think about it, since everyone else says I look fine. But... no, not really. I just can't see how I could be completely wrong when it's right there on my face. It feels too real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have your concerns about your hairline and face changed at all over time, or have they stayed pretty much the same?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean... I don't think they've changed much. It feels like it's just always been this way. Day in and day out, it's just the exact same things I'm focusing on. So, yeah, I'd say it's stayed pretty much the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance that your hairline and face actually look normal, and it's just your thoughts about them that are distorted?\n\nPatient: I... honestly, I don't really know how to answer that anymore. We just talked about this, right? I mean, I guess it's the same answer. People tell me it's distorted, so maybe there's a chance they're right. But... when I look in the mirror, it just doesn't look normal to me. It's just hard to believe it's only in my head when I can see it so clearly.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social interactions or activities outside of work because of your concerns about your hairline and face?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I am. It's actually affecting a lot. I mean, at the restaurant where I host, I've actually been written up recently because I end up being late or missing my shifts entirely... I just get stuck trying to fix how I look and can't leave the house. \n\nAnd outside of work, yeah, I've stopped doing pretty much everything. I don't go to gallery events anymore, or go on hikes like I used to. I even avoid any staff photos at work. I just... I really can't handle being seen looking this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you didn't think about your hairline and face, or when those concerns seemed less important to you?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. I mean, not that I can remember. It feels like this has just been... always there. I can't really think of a time when I wasn't worried about it, or when it didn't feel like a big deal. It's just always on my mind.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean, I guess it's possible, since everyone keeps telling me it's fine. But... it's just really hard for me to believe that. When I look in the mirror, it doesn't look normal to me at all. It just looks wrong. So even if it is possible, it's just not what I see.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your hairline and face could be completely wrong, even if it feels absolutely right to you?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess maybe? If I really force myself to think about it, since everyone else says I look fine. But... no, not really. I just can't see how I could be completely wrong when it's right there on my face. It feels too real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have your concerns about your hairline and face changed at all over time, or have they stayed pretty much the same?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean... I don't think they've changed much. It feels like it's just always been this way. Day in and day out, it's just the exact same things I'm focusing on. So, yeah, I'd say it's stayed pretty much the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance that your hairline and face actually look normal, and it's just your thoughts about them that are distorted?\n\nPatient: I... honestly, I don't really know how to answer that anymore. We just talked about this, right? I mean, I guess it's the same answer. People tell me it's distorted, so maybe there's a chance they're right. But... when I look in the mirror, it just doesn't look normal to me. It's just hard to believe it's only in my head when I can see it so clearly.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social interactions or activities outside of work because of your concerns about your hairline and face?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I am. It's actually affecting a lot. I mean, at the restaurant where I host, I've actually been written up recently because I end up being late or missing my shifts entirely... I just get stuck trying to fix how I look and can't leave the house. \n\nAnd outside of work, yeah, I've stopped doing pretty much everything. I don't go to gallery events anymore, or go on hikes like I used to. I even avoid any staff photos at work. I just... I really can't handle being seen looking this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you didn't think about your hairline and face, or when those concerns seemed less important to you?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. I mean, not that I can remember. It feels like this has just been... always there. I can't really think of a time when I wasn't worried about it, or when it didn't feel like a big deal. It's just always on my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your appearance could be considered an overestimation or exaggeration of a minor flaw, rather than an accurate assessment of how you look?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't think so. I mean, I know you and other people might think it's an exaggeration, but to me, it's not minor at all. It's not an overestimation. When I look in the mirror, it's... it's just exactly what I see. It feels very real and very noticeable.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I'm here because I just... I can't stop thinking about my hairline and the way my face looks. I work as a host at a restaurant, and when I'm standing under those lights, I feel like I look incredibly old and just... strange. It's really bothering me. My coworkers keep telling me they don't see what I'm talking about, but I just can't stop focusing on it.\n\nDoctor: Is it just your hairline and face that you're concerned about, or are there other parts of your body that you worry about as well?\n\nPatient: No, it's really just my hairline and... and my face. I don't really worry about the rest of my body at all. It's just everything from the neck up that feels so wrong. But yeah, no other parts, just my face and my hair.\n\nDoctor: Is this concern about your hairline and face affecting your work or daily activities in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it really is. I've actually been written up at the restaurant recently because I keep being late, or I just miss my shifts entirely. And outside of work, I've pretty much stopped doing things. I won't be in any of the staff photos anymore, and I've stopped going to gallery events or out on hikes like I used to. I just... I really can't handle being seen that way by other people.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your appearance is actually abnormal, or is it just that you feel self-conscious about it?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... to me, it really does look abnormal. I know my coworkers keep saying they don't see it, but when I look in the mirror, it's just... it's right there. It doesn't feel like I'm just being self-conscious or overly picky. My hairline and my face just genuinely look wrong to me. So, yeah, I really do think it actually looks that bad, even if other people try to tell me it doesn't.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people can see the flaws in your face and hairline that you're concerned about?\n\nPatient: Well, my coworkers tell me they don't see what I'm talking about when I bring it up. But... I just can't stop thinking about it. That's actually why I'm here. I feel like when I'm at work, under those bright restaurant lights, my hairline and my face just make me look so old and strange. So even though people say they don't notice it, I feel like they have to be able to see it. I just can't stop focusing on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your hairline and face actually look normal, but you just can't see them that way?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean, I guess it's possible, since everyone keeps telling me it's fine. But... it's just really hard for me to believe that. When I look in the mirror, it doesn't look normal to me at all. It just looks wrong. So even if it is possible, it's just not what I see.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your hairline and face could be completely wrong, even if it feels absolutely right to you?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess maybe? If I really force myself to think about it, since everyone else says I look fine. But... no, not really. I just can't see how I could be completely wrong when it's right there on my face. It feels too real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have your concerns about your hairline and face changed at all over time, or have they stayed pretty much the same?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean... I don't think they've changed much. It feels like it's just always been this way. Day in and day out, it's just the exact same things I'm focusing on. So, yeah, I'd say it's stayed pretty much the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance that your hairline and face actually look normal, and it's just your thoughts about them that are distorted?\n\nPatient: I... honestly, I don't really know how to answer that anymore. We just talked about this, right? I mean, I guess it's the same answer. People tell me it's distorted, so maybe there's a chance they're right. But... when I look in the mirror, it just doesn't look normal to me. It's just hard to believe it's only in my head when I can see it so clearly.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social interactions or activities outside of work because of your concerns about your hairline and face?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I am. It's actually affecting a lot. I mean, at the restaurant where I host, I've actually been written up recently because I end up being late or missing my shifts entirely... I just get stuck trying to fix how I look and can't leave the house. \n\nAnd outside of work, yeah, I've stopped doing pretty much everything. I don't go to gallery events anymore, or go on hikes like I used to. I even avoid any staff photos at work. I just... I really can't handle being seen looking this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you didn't think about your hairline and face, or when those concerns seemed less important to you?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. I mean, not that I can remember. It feels like this has just been... always there. I can't really think of a time when I wasn't worried about it, or when it didn't feel like a big deal. It's just always on my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your appearance could be considered an overestimation or exaggeration of a minor flaw, rather than an accurate assessment of how you look?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't think so. I mean, I know you and other people might think it's an exaggeration, but to me, it's not minor at all. It's not an overestimation. I know what a normal hairline and a normal face look like, and... mine just isn't. I'm not misinterpreting anything, I'm just seeing what's actually there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your hairline and face could improve or change in a way that would make you feel better about your appearance?\n\nPatient: I don't really see how they could improve, to be honest. I just don't see that happening. I'm 54 years old, and I've looked at myself every single day. I know my coworkers keep saying they don't see it, but when I look in the mirror, it's just... it's right there. It doesn't feel like I'm just being self-conscious or overly picky. My hairline and my face just genuinely look wrong to me. So, yeah, I really do think it actually looks that bad, even if other people try to tell me it doesn't.\n\nDoctor: Do you think other people can see the flaws in your face and hairline that you're concerned about?\n\nPatient: Well, my coworkers tell me they don't see what I'm talking about when I bring it up. But... I just can't stop thinking about it. That's actually why I'm here. I feel like when I'm at work, under those bright restaurant lights, my hairline and my face just make me look so old and strange. So even though people say they don't notice it, I feel like they have to be able to see it. I just can't stop focusing on it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your hairline and face actually look normal, but you just can't see them that way?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I mean, I guess it's possible, since everyone keeps telling me it's fine. But... it's just really hard for me to believe that. When I look in the mirror, it doesn't look normal to me at all. It just looks wrong. So even if it is possible, it's just not what I see.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your hairline and face could be completely wrong, even if it feels absolutely right to you?\n\nPatient: I mean... I guess maybe? If I really force myself to think about it, since everyone else says I look fine. But... no, not really. I just can't see how I could be completely wrong when it's right there on my face. It feels too real to me.\n\nDoctor: Have your concerns about your hairline and face changed at all over time, or have they stayed pretty much the same?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I mean... I don't think they've changed much. It feels like it's just always been this way. Day in and day out, it's just the exact same things I'm focusing on. So, yeah, I'd say it's stayed pretty much the same.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance that your hairline and face actually look normal, and it's just your thoughts about them that are distorted?\n\nPatient: I... honestly, I don't really know how to answer that anymore. We just talked about this, right? I mean, I guess it's the same answer. People tell me it's distorted, so maybe there's a chance they're right. But... when I look in the mirror, it just doesn't look normal to me. It's just hard to believe it's only in my head when I can see it so clearly.\n\nDoctor: Are you avoiding social interactions or activities outside of work because of your concerns about your hairline and face?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I am. It's actually affecting a lot. I mean, at the restaurant where I host, I've actually been written up recently because I end up being late or missing my shifts entirely... I just get stuck trying to fix how I look and can't leave the house. \n\nAnd outside of work, yeah, I've stopped doing pretty much everything. I don't go to gallery events anymore, or go on hikes like I used to. I even avoid any staff photos at work. I just... I really can't handle being seen looking this way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you didn't think about your hairline and face, or when those concerns seemed less important to you?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. I mean, not that I can remember. It feels like this has just been... always there. I can't really think of a time when I wasn't worried about it, or when it didn't feel like a big deal. It's just always on my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concerns about your appearance could be considered an overestimation or exaggeration of a minor flaw, rather than an accurate assessment of how you look?\n\nPatient: I... I really don't think so. I mean, I know you and other people might think it's an exaggeration, but to me, it's not minor at all. It's not an overestimation. When I look in the mirror, it's... it's just exactly what I see. It feels very real and very noticeable.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your hairline and face are actually normal, but you're misinterpreting how they look?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't see how that's possible. I mean, I look at myself every day. I see the exact same thing every time. It's not like my eyes are playing tricks on me. I know what a normal hairline and a normal face look like, and... mine just isn't. I'm not misinterpreting anything, I'm just seeing what's actually there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your hairline and face could improve or change in a way that would make you feel better about your appearance?\n\nPatient: I don't really see how they could improve, to be honest. I mean, it's my bone structure and my hair... it's not like a haircut or washing my face differently is going to fix the core problem. Unless I got some kind of major surgery, which I can't afford, this is just... this is just how it is. So, no, I haven't really considered that they'll just naturally get better.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your views about your hairline and face could ever change over time?\n\nPatient: I just don't see that happening. I'm 54 years old, and I've looked at myself every single day. The way my hairline is receding and the shape of my face... it's not something my mind is just making up, so I don't see why my view of it would change. Unless the physical reality of my face somehow changes, which it won't, then no, I don't think my views will change. It is what it is, and I have to live with looking like this.\n\nDoctor: Do you think someone else could look at you and not notice anything out of the ordinary with your hairline and face?\n\nPatient: I mean... my coworkers actually tell me they don't see what I'm talking about. When I'm at work, standing under those harsh restaurant lights, I can just see exactly how my hairline and my face make me look so old and strange. Even if the people I work with tell me they don't see it, it's really all I can focus on.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there is any situation or circumstance that could make you feel better about your hairline and face?\n\nPatient: I really don't know. I mean... I guess if I could somehow afford some sort of major cosmetic surgery, maybe that would change things. But short of that, no. I can't think of any situation that would just make me feel better about it. It's my face, it's right there in the mirror every day. I don't see how a different situation is going to change what I see.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I just get stuck trying to fix how I look and can't leave the house. \n\nAnd outside of work, yeah, I've stopped doing pretty much everything. I really don't think so. I mean, I know you and other people might think it's an exaggeration, but to me, it's not minor at all. It's not an overestimation. When I look in the mirror, it's... it's just exactly what I see. It feels very real and very noticeable.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that your hairline and face are actually normal, but you're misinterpreting how they look?\n\nPatient: I don't... I don't see how that's possible. I mean, I look at myself every day. I see the exact same thing every time. It's not like my eyes are playing tricks on me. I know what a normal hairline and a normal face look like, and... mine just isn't. I'm not misinterpreting anything, I'm just seeing what's actually there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that your hairline and face could improve or change in a way that would make you feel better about your appearance?\n\nPatient: I don't really see how they could improve, to be honest. I mean, it's my bone structure and my hair... it's not like a haircut or washing my face differently is going to fix the core problem. Unless I got some kind of major surgery, which I can't afford, this is just... this is just how it is. So, no, I haven't really considered that they'll just naturally get better.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your views about your hairline and face could ever change over time?\n\nPatient: I just don't see that happening. The way my hairline is receding and the shape of my face... it's not something my mind is just making up, so I don't see why my view of it would change.
